> **CONDUCTOR HARD RULE — PR REVIEW AUTOMATION (operator directive
> 2026-05-10):** at the START of every tick, after the queue-depth
> check, count open PRs (`gh pr list --state open --json number --jq
> '. | length'`). If **> 5**, spawn a `general-purpose` review agent
> to triage them in parallel with the rest of the tick. The agent's
> brief: per-PR status (mergeable/conflicting/stale), recommendation
> (MERGE-NOW / FIX-with-recipe / CLOSE-AS-OBSOLETE / ESCALATE), under
> 250 words per PR, total report under 1000 words. Conductor reads the
> report and actions it (merge clean PRs, post fix-requests, close
> obsolete, escalate as needed). This stops the long-tail of stale
> PRs from accumulating unmonitored. The agent runs read-only — no
> merges, no closes, no code changes; the conductor reserves all
> mutation authority.

> **CONDUCTOR HARD RULE — ZOMBIE-CLAIM SWEEP (multi-machine
> amendment 2026-05-10):** workers may run on multiple machines
> (Mac/PC/Linux); pulse files at `/tmp/argo-worker-pulse/<brief-id>`
> are LOCAL-ONLY signals. The conductor's prior pulse-presence
> check produces false-positive zombie demotions when a worker is
> alive on a different machine. **New rule:** a CLAIMED row is a
> zombie ONLY if BOTH (a) its `claimed_at` timestamp in the row
> note is **>2h** old AND (b) no PR exists on the branch AND no
> recent push to the branch in the last 30min. Pulse-file presence
> is informational ONLY (sufficient evidence of alive, but absence
> is NOT evidence of dead). When in doubt, leave CLAIMED — workers
> have HARD RULE 7 race-loss recovery and will release their own
> claim if they hit the 3-attempt-fail rule. **Conductor singleton
> rule:** exactly ONE conductor process across all machines. Two
> conductors WILL race on ledger flips and corrupt rows. Workers
> can run on N machines; conductor cannot.

Status values: `TODO` (claim me) · `CLAIMED` (someone is
working) · `IN_REVIEW` (PR open) · `MERGED` (on main) ·
`BLOCKED` (needs unblock).

When you claim or update a row, commit BUILD_STATUS.md with
message `status: <action> <brief-id>` (e.g. `status: claim
C-gmail`, `status: C-gmail in review (#812)`).

(1) `crates/argo-skill/src/registry.rs` does not exist — never has, per `git log`. The actual skill registry is `argo-skill/src/store.rs` (`SkillStore` over `argo-store::SessionStore`). Freshness check `scripts/check-brief-freshness.sh` flags the missing file. (2) No `min_trust_grade` field exists on skill rows — grep across `crates/argo-skill/` returns 0 matches. The brief asserts "Skill rows already carry a `min_trust_grade` field (per #948 schema); reuse, do not extend schema" — but #948 (commit 13233c4a) added only `body_source: Option<String>` to `SkillCandidate` and stamped `body_source=dreams_distilled`; it did NOT add `min_trust_grade` to the skill schema or any other table. (3) No worker↔trust-grade concept exists. `TrustGrade` (in `argo-skill/src/trust.rs`) is derived from skill **provenance** via `from_source(provenance.source)` — a SKILL-side attribute. The risk-tier→min-trust mapping in `min_trust_for_risk("low"/"medium"/"high")` keys off ACTION risk, not worker trust. The brief's data model ("Worker B trust_grade=L2", "Worker C trust_grade=L1") has no analogue in `Worker` / `IdentityStore` / `argo-identity` (the sibling mission-routing-skillmatch-rolematch BLOCK flagged the same gap). (4) Brief's must-NOT-touch list explicitly forbids modifying skill schema/migrations — exactly the surface a real implementation must extend to add `min_trust_grade` (skill-side) AND a worker `trust_grade` column (worker-side). Internally contradictory. (5) `SkillDiscovery::load_for_session(worker_id)` and `SkillRegistry::available_for_worker(worker_id, trust_grade)` are both pure brief inventions — grep across the workspace returns matches only in this brief and the sibling routing brief. (6) `crates/argo-cli/src/handlers/worker_actions.rs` has no `boot`, `session load`, or `action vocab` token matching the hand-wave at "boot-time path where the worker's available action vocab is loaded" — the brief presumes a wiring point that isn't there. **Conductor**: please rewrite as a multi-step package: (a) skill schema migration adding `min_trust_grade` column, (b) worker schema migration adding `trust_grade` column to `IdentityStore::Worker`, (c) `SkillStore::available_for_worker` lookup against the new columns, (d) wire-in brief that identifies the actual worker session-boot path in `argo-cli` or `argo-agent-runner` (currently unidentified). Each prerequisite warrants its own brief; the cross-worker propagation integration test should be the LAST step once (a)-(d) are MERGED. Note: the v0.2 build-plan §H-4a comment in `trust.rs` flags this as a planned follow-up ("the two-tier workspace→library walk and the skill-learning loop ... land in follow-up slices that depend on this type") — but those slices haven't landed; the schema groundwork in this brief is the missing prep work. **🎯 Tier M6 (mission MVP critical path) — NEW 2026-05-10 by conductor (operator strategic brief).** Brief: streams/mission-skill-propagation-loop.md. |

## Picking your next brief

All agent families pick the first row where:
- `status == TODO`
- All "depends on" / "waits on" predecessors have `status == MERGED`

The owner column is a sizing/provenance hint, not a hard lock, unless
the live protocol in `CLAUDE.md` says otherwise.

If multiple agents try to claim the same row, the one whose
commit lands first on main wins. The losers will see the row is
already CLAIMED on their next pull and pick a different one.

---

## Blanket CLI allowlist (tick 32 amendment)

Repeated BLOCKs from may-touch lists that allow a NEW file
but forbid the EXISTING dispatch wiring. Going forward, every
CLI-extension brief MAY also touch:

- `crates/argo-cli/src/cli.rs` — narrowly: ONE arm in the
  Command enum + ONE dispatch arm
- `crates/argo-cli/src/main.rs` — narrowly: ONE handler call
- `crates/argo-cli/src/handlers/mod.rs` — narrowly: ONE
  `pub mod <name>;` line
- `crates/argo-cli/tests/<name>.rs` — NEW integration test file
- `crates/argo-cli/Cargo.toml` — ONE line for any new path
  dep on a sibling crate

Strict scope still applies: ONE arm/line per file. Don't
refactor unrelated logic. The blanket unblocks tier-2 CLI
briefs without per-brief amendments.

This applies retroactively to any BLOCKED brief flagging
"dispatch file outside allowlist" — codex may resume those
briefs in fresh worktrees on origin/main.

## Tick 45 amendment: exhaustive-match propagation

When a brief adds a new enum variant (e.g.
`HandoffStatus::Cancelled`, `MemoryCmd::SharedTeam`),
Rust's exhaustiveness check forces every existing `match`
on that enum across the workspace to handle the new arm.
Those adjacent files are AUTOMATICALLY may-touch under
strict scope: ONLY the new arm (usually one line; use
`_ => ...` if no bespoke handling is needed).

Specifically: `gateway/routes_orchestrator.rs`,
`handlers/memory.rs`, `worker_actions.rs`, and any other
handler that pattern-matches on the enum being extended
is may-touch — only the new arm; do not refactor.

Default action: fix the exhaustive match in place.

---

## Tick 115 amendment: claim-collision defenses

Three collision patterns observed and codified:

1. **Auto-compaction-amnesia** — a tab finishes a brief, gets
   auto-compacted, comes back not knowing its own work shipped,
   re-implements the same brief. **Defense**: worker prompts
   now include a PRE-PUSH FLIGHT CHECK (step 8) — re-pull main
   + grep BUILD_STATUS for the brief-id before pushing the
   feature branch. If MERGED → graceful exit. Stops 90% of
   duplicate-PR situations.

2. **False-positive stale-sweep + late recovery** — conductor
   sweeps a 30-min CLAIMED row to TODO, fresh tab claims, but
   the original tab was alive in slow build/test, eventually
   pushes a PR → conflict. **Defense**: stale-sweep threshold
   bumped from 30 min to **45 min**. Big briefs (architectural,
   multi-crate) legitimately need 30+ min for cargo build +
   test + clippy; 45-min threshold reduces false positives.

3. **Simultaneous next-claim race** — two tabs finish
   near-concurrently, claim the same next-TODO row, race
   resolved by first-push-wins. **Defenses**:
   (a) Worker prompts now include ITERATION JITTER (step 12) —
   sleep random 5-30s before next claim, spreads tabs out.
   (b) RACE-LOST HANDLING (step 3) is more explicit: pull,
   re-read ledger, claim a DIFFERENT row (never retry the
   same one).

**Conductor habit (tick 115)**: when manually opening a PR for
a stalled worker (worker pushed branch but didn't `gh pr create`),
add a note to the BUILD_STATUS row: "conductor-opened; original
tab should self-exit on next ledger re-read". This way the
original tab's RULE 3 check (don't reimplement merged briefs)
fires correctly post-compaction.
